PROJECT MONITORING; RECORDS AND AUDIT RIGHTS 
Section 7.01 Project Monitoring 
The ACA and any of its authorized representatives may monitor and inspect all phases and 
aspects of the Subrecipient’s performance with respect to the Project to determine compliance 
with the requirements of this Agreement, the proper use of funds, and other technical and 
administrative requirements of this Agreement, including the adequacy of the Subrecipient’s 
records and accounts. 
Section 7.02 Records and Audit Rights 
(a) The Subrecipient shall create and maintain records supporting the services 
covered by this Agreement, including but not limited to, financial records  (including an 
accounting system of procedures and practices that conf orms to GAAP, as defined in the 
Uniform Requirements), supporting documents, and such other records as are required by law or 
other authority, for a period of five (5) years after either the expiration or earlier termination of 
the Term  or the conclusion of any claim, litigation, or exception relating to th is Agreement, 
whichever is later. Such records will be kept in the Subrecipient’s offices. 
(b) The Subrecipient shall make available to the ACA, the Treasury Office of 
Inspector General, the Government Accountability Office, Treasury,  and their authorized 
representatives, and allow each of them access to, such books, records , and documents as they 
may reasonably request in connection with the Project,  including, but not limited to , monitoring 
compliance with the terms and conditions of this Agreement.  The ACA shall pr ovide the 
Subrecipient forty-eight (48) hours’ written notice prior to exercising its rights to access and 
audit the Subrecipient’s books, records, and documents pursuant to this Section 7.02(b)  and 
Section 7.02(c); provided, however, the ACA may exercise such rights unannounced and without 
prior notice during a Subrecipient Default or when there is good faith suspicion of fraud. 
(c) The ACA, at the ACA’s own expense, will have the right to carry out an audit of 
information relating to this Agreem ent. Such audit may extend, without limitation, to 
calculations undertaken, and financial or business reports provided by or on behalf of, the 
Subrecipient pursuant to this Agreement.  The ACA and its employees, agents, auditors, 
attorneys, and consultants,  at the ACA’s own expense, may examine, copy, take extracts from, 
and audit all the books and records of the Subrecipient related to the Project, including all 
subcontracts entered into  by the Subrecipient. The ACA and its agents, auditors, attorneys, and

operations of the Subrecipient to confirm the accuracy of books and records.

ARTICLE 9. 
DEFAULT; TERMINATION 
Section 9.01 Subrecipient Defaults 
The occurrence of any one or more of the following will constitute a “Subrecipient Default:” 
(a) the Subrecipient breaches any material obligation under this Agreement; 
(b) the Subrecipient fails to comply with any governmental approval o r law 
applicable to the Grant or the Project in any material respect; 
(c) the Subrecipient makes any intentionally unt rue statement or materially 
misleading certification in any Reimbursement Request  or other report required to be submitted 
to the ACA pursuant to the terms of this Agreement;

<!-- page 339 -->
ACA Grant Agreement ABDG22- R05     
Page 15 
 
(d) the Subrecipient expends Grant proceeds on costs that are not Allowable Project 
Costs; 
(e) any representation or warranty made by the Subrecipient in this Agreement or any 
certificate, schedule, report, or instrument or other document delivered by the Subrecipient to the 
ACA is false or materially misleading or inaccurate when made, in each case in any material 
respect, or omits material information when made; 
(f) the Subrecipient fails to keep adequate records, including the failure to document 
Grant funding expenditures, or otherwise fails to comply with the accounting and reporting 
requirements set forth in this Agreement; 
(g) the Subrecipient fails to begin the applicable portions of the Project within thirty 
(30) days of the Effective Date; 
(h) the abandonment of the Project by the Subrecipient; 
(i) the Subrecipient makes any written repudiation of this Agreement; 
(j) the Subrecipient fails to comply with Section 10.03 (Assignment by Subrecipient); 
or 
(k) the Subrecipient’s bankruptc y or insolvency or the dissolution or liquidation of 
the Subrecipient’s business organization or assets. 
Section 9.02 Notice and Cure Periods (Subrecipient Defaults) 
(a) The ACA shall provide written notice (“ Subrecipient Default Notice ”) to the 
Subrecipient upon the occurrence of a Subrecipient Default. 
(b) The Subrecipient shall have a period of thirty (30) days from the date of any 
Subrecipient Default Notice to cure the Subrecipient Default(s) described therein . 
Notwithstanding the foregoing, upon the occurre nce of a Subrecipient Default described in 
Section 9.01(k) (Subrecipient Defaults), the ACA’s right to terminate this Agreement shall be 
immediate, without a notice and cure period. 
(c) A Subrecipient Default under Section 9.01(e)  (Subrecipient Defaults) shall be 
regarded as cured when the adverse effects of such Subrecipient Default are cured  by written 
confirmation of the ACA. 
Section 9.03 Termination for Subrecipient Default 
(a) If a Subrecipient Default occurs and the Subrecipient Default has not been cured 
within any relevant cure period set out in Section 9.02 (Notice and Cure Periods  (Subrecipient 
Defaults)), the ACA may serve a termination notice (“ ACA Termination Notice ”) on the 
Subrecipient at any time during the continuance of such Subrecipient Default.

<!-- page 340 -->
ACA Grant Agreement ABDG22- R05     
Page 16 
 
(b) The ACA Termination Notice must specify the Subrecipient Default that has 
occurred entitling the ACA to terminate. 
Section 9.04 Compensation on Termination (Subrecipient Defaults) 
(a) If this Agreement is terminated in accordance with Section 9.03 (Termination for 
Subrecipient Default), the Subrecipient shall pay to the ACA an amount equal to: 
(i) the total amount of funds disbursed by the ACA to the Subrecipient under 
this Agreement and 
(ii) any other losses arising directly from such termination, subject to the 
limitations set forth in Section 10.08 (Limitation on Consequential Damages). 
(b) The Subrecipient shall pay the sum described in Section 9.04(a) within sixty (60) 
days after such amount is finally agreed or determined. 
Section 9.05 Termination for Convenience 
(a) The ACA may terminate this Agreement  in accordance with this Section 9.05 at 
any time the ACA determines that such termination is in the best interest of the ACA or the 
State. 
(b) If the ACA wishes to terminate this Agreement under this Section 9.05, it shall 
deliver a termination notice to the Subrecipient stating: 
(i) that the ACA is terminating this Agreement under this Section 9.05; and 
(ii) that this Agreement will terminate on the date specified in the termination 
notice, which must be a minimum of thirty (30) days after the date the Subrecipient 
receives such termination notice. 
(c) This Agreement will terminate on the date specified in the termination notice 
referred to in Section 9.05(b). 
Section 9.06 Compensation on Termination (For Convenience) 
If this Agreement is termina ted pursuant to Section 9.05  (Termination for Convenience ), the 
ACA shall pay to the Subrecipient all reasonable and documented Allowable Project Costs, for 
which the Subrecipient has not been previously reimbursed, incurred by the Subrecipient prior to 
the date of the termination notice referred to in Section 9.05(b) (Termination for Convenience ); 
provided that in no event shall the amount payable by the ACA pursuant to this Section 9.06, 
together with all amounts previously paid by the ACA to the Subrecipient pursuant to the terms 
of this Agreement, exceed the amount of the Grant.

applicable law are waived to the maximum extent permitted by applicable law.

Section 10.05 Binding Effect 
Subject to the limitations of Section 10.03 (Assignment by Subrecipient), this Agreement will be 
binding upon  and will inure to the benefit of the Parties hereto and their respective legal 
representatives, successors, and permitted assigns, and wherever a reference in this Agreement is 
made to any of the Parties hereto, such reference also will be deemed to inclu de, wherever 
applicable, a reference to the legal representatives, successors and permitted assigns of such 
Party, as if in every case so expressed. 
Section 10.06 Relationship of Parties 
(a) The relationship of the Subrecipient to the ACA will be one of a recipient of grant 
funds acting as an independent contractor , not an agent, partner, lessee, joint or co -venturer or 
employee, and neither the ACA nor the Subrecipient will have any rights to direct or control the 
activities of the other or their respecti ve affiliates, subcontractors, or consultants, except as 
otherwise expressly provided in this Agreement. 
(b) Officials, employees, and agents of the Subrecipient or the ACA will in no event 
be considered employees, agents, partners or representatives of the other. 
Section 10.07 No Third-Party Beneficiaries 
Nothing contained in this Agreement is intended or shall be construed as creating or conferring 
any rights, benefits or remedies upon, or creating any obligations of the Parties hereto toward, 
any person or entity not a party to this Agreement. 
Section 10.08 Limitation on Consequential Damages 
Except as expressly provided in this Agreement to the contrary, neither Party shall be liable to 
the other for punitive damages or special, indirect, incidental or consequential damages of any 
nature, whether arising in contract, tort (including negligence) or other legal theory. 
Section 10.09 Disputes 
(a) The Parties agree to use arbitration in the event of disputes in accordance with the 
provisions of A.R.S. §§ 12-1501-12-1518 and the terms set forth herein . The laws of the State 
apply to questions arising under this Agreement and any litigation regarding this Agreement 
must be maintained in State courts, except as pertaining to disputes which are subject to 
arbitration. 
(b) In the event of a dispute, controversy, or cl aim arising out of or relating in any 
way to this Agreement, the complaining Party shall notify the other Party in writing thereof.

<!-- page 344 -->
ACA Grant Agreement ABDG22- R05     
Page 20 
 
Within thirty (30) days of such notice, representatives of both Parties shall meet at an agreed 
location to attempt to resolve the dispute in good faith. Should the dispute not be resolved within 
thirty (30) days after such notice, the complaining Party shall seek remedies exclusively through 
arbitration, in accordance with the provisions of A.R.S. §§ 12-1501 through 12-1518. 
(c) The arbitration demand must be a clear and concise statement of the claim or 
dispute. The respondent’s answer and any counterclaims must be filed within twenty (20) days of 
service of the demand. 
(d) The arbitration shall be conducted in Maricopa County, Arizona. 
(e) The arbitration shall be conducted by one (1) arbitrator. If the Parties are not able 
to agree upon the selection of an arbitrator, within twenty (20) days of the commencement of an 
arbitration proceeding by service of a demand for arbitration, the cour t on application of a Party 
shall appoint the arbitrator. 
(f) The arbitrator must promptly set a conference to clarify the claims and defenses, 
to establish fair procedures, and to set a schedule for completing the arbitration. 
(g) It is the intent of the Parties that, barring extraordinary circumstances, arbitration 
proceedings will be concluded within one hundred twenty (120) days from the date the arbitrator 
is appointed. The arbitrator may extend the time limit in the interests of justice. Failure to ad here 
to this time limit shall not constitute a basis for challenging the award. 
(h) The arbitrator must issue a written, reasoned award within twenty (20) days from 
the date the hearing is formally closed, or as soon after that as is feasible. The sole remedy will 
be actual damages; no punitive damages are allowed. 
(i) Unless the arbitrator orders otherwise, the Parties must share arbitration costs 
equally, including arbitrator’s fees and expenses. Each Party must pay its own expenses and 
attorney’s fees. 
Section 10.10 Recission of Funding by Treasury 
The ACA shall have no obligation to make disbursements of the Grant to the Subrecipient in the 
event and to the extent that any funding for the Grant provided by Treasury is rescinded by 
Treasury or otherwise becomes unavailable to the State for any reason.  
Section 10.11 ACA Monetary Obligations 
All monetary obligations of the ACA under this Agreement ar e subject to appropriation by the 
Arizona Legislature; provided, however , that in the absence of such appropriation, such 
monetary obligations shall be payable solely from other unencumbered lawfully available funds 
of the ACA (whether available at such ti me or in the future) that are not funds appropriated by 
the Arizona Legislature. The ACA will submit a request in accordance with applicable law from 
the Arizona Legislature, or shall perform actions permitted by applicable law to obtain, 
designate, or use  any other lawfully available funds that are not funds appropriated by the 
Arizona Legislature. This Agreement does not create a debt under the Arizona Constitution.

<!-- page 345 -->

Section 10.12 Survival 
All provisions  of this Agreement that by their inherent character should survive expiration or 
earlier termination of this Agreement , including, but not limited to, the Continuing Obligations , 
Section 10.02  (Disclaimer; Indemnification ), and Section 10.09  (Disputes), will survive the 
expiration or earlier termination of this Agreement. 
Section 10.13 Counterparts 
This Agreement may be executed in two (2) or more counterparts, each of which will be deemed 
an original, but all of which together will constitute one and the same instrument. 
Section 10.14 Entire Agreement; Amendment 
This Agreement constitutes the entire and exclusive agreement between the Parties relating to the 
specific matters covered herein and therein. All prior written and prior or contemporaneous 
verbal agreements, understandings, representations and/or practices relative to the foregoing, 
including any interim agreement, are hereby superseded, revoked, and ren dered ineffective for 
any purpose. This Agreement may be altered, amended, or revoked only by an instrument in 
writing signed by each Party hereto, or its permitted successor or assignee. No verbal agreement 
or implied covenant will be held to vary the ter ms hereof, any statute, law or custom to the 
contrary notwithstanding.

EXHIBIT C 
PROJECT SUMMARY AND BUDGET 
Project Name: Navajo County Middle Mile 
Rural/Urban: Rural 
County: Navajo 
Communities Households Population Business Anchor 
7 N/A 57,506 1,297 77 
Project Summary: 
Navajo County plans to construct a County-owned middle-mile network that will connect several 
communities along Highways 77 and 260. This open-access middle-mile fiber infrastructure will 
lower the cost of investment for new internet service providers looking to enter the market by 
enabling them to use County-owned infrastructure to access communities in Navajo County. The 
service providers can use the County’s middle-mile fiber infrastructure as a jumping off point to 
make last-mile connections that will reach residents and businesses in each of the communities 
that the middle-mile fiber runs through. The infrastructure will be leased to these internet service 
providers on a non-exclusive, non-discriminatory basis, allowing the County to increase options 
for highspeed internet for its residents and businesses. Service providers using the network will 
be required to participate in Federal low-cost internet programs for qualifying residents. The 
network will also connect County facilities, ensuring continuity of operations and enabling 
critical public safety communications. The middle-mile network will create resiliency and 
redundancy both within the County and throughout the region, providing reliable connections to 
ensure Navajo County’s residents and businesses have the high-speed internet they need.

age 1 of 4 
Federal Regulatory Requirements Checklist 
Capital Projects Fund  
Arizona Broadband Development Grant Program 
July 2023  
Disclaimer: Guidance provided in this document is meant to be used as a helpful resource but does not assure 
compliance with all state and federal requirements. Federal requirements included here are those that may be of 
particular importance to subrecipients  and/or contractors  receiving Capital Projects Fund (CPF) for broadband 
programs. The descriptions below are only general summaries and all recipients , subrecipients and contractors 
are advised to carefully review the full requirements as well  as any additional regulatory and statutory 
requirements applicable to the program.  ACA Broadband Office may revise these requirements based on 
supplemental guidance provided the U.S. Treasury and/or the State of Arizona . 
1. Contract and Award
1.1 Applicability Under 2 C.F.R. Part 200.101, the terms and conditions of federal awards 
generally flow down to subcontracts and subawards. This means that 
those receiving federal funding must comply with requirements 
regardless of whether they are a recipient or subrecipient of a federal 
award.  
Co
nfirm with ACA Broadband Office if your organization is classified as 
a subrecipient or a contractor under the CPF award. This classification 
determines which provisions in the Uniform Guidance will apply. 
I
f the Internet Service Provider (ISP) is treated as a subrecipient, the 
Uniform Guidance provisions applied to subrecipients must be followed 
subject to the additional guidance provided by the Treasury on May 17, 
2023, on property, program income, cost principles, procurement, 
audit, and monitoring requirements.  
I
f the ISP is treated as a contractor, the provisions in the Uniform 
Guidance stating rules on contractors must be adhered to, including 
procurement rules for selecting contractors and imposing applicable 
terms in Appendix II of the Uniform Guidance. 
☐
1.2 Award Price 
Structure  
This award is a cost-reimbursement grant. Entities that receive fixed 
amount subawards, or ISPs treated as subcontractor, are not required 
to comply with the cost principles and procurement practices of the 
Uniform Guidance.  
"
Fixed amount subaward" includes subawards that may have a cost-
sharing or match requirement or where the ISP must provide evidence 
of costs (for example, maximum payment amounts based on estimates 
of actual costs). 
☐

<!-- page 356 -->
Page 2 of 4 
 
An entity receiving a fixed amount subaward must certify to the 
recipient that the broadband project reached substantial completion 1 
by the end of the period of performance, December 31, 2026. Costs 
incurred prior to an ISP receiving an award are reimbursable to the  
extent that they would have been allowable, for example, if an ISP 
purchased fiber or other broadband equipment in advance of being 
awarded a subaward or contract. 
1.3 Real Property and 
Equipment 
Confirm if your grant agreement includes that title to real property or 
equipment acquired or improved under this grant award vests to your 
organization upon completion of the project.  
 
Treasury has provided modified requirements for property associated 
with fixed award  broadband infrastructure projects funded with CPF 
and substantially completed by December 31, 2026, instead of applying 
the full Uniform Guidance. In this case, the federal interest in the 
infrastructure will last until December 31, 2034 (Federal  
Interest Period). Review the additional guidance provided by Treasury 
for the conditions: SLFRF and CPF Supplementary Broadband Guidance 
(treasury.gov)  
☐ 
 
1.4 Suspension and 
Debarment 
Requirements 
Provide documentation confirming your organization and your 
subcontractors are not suspended, debarred, or otherwise excluded 
prior to contract execution. 
☐ 
2. Procurement and Project Costs 
2.1 Procurement 
Standards (not applicable 
to fixed amount awards) 
Review the procurement requirements for the acquisition of property 
or services required under a federal award or subaward, per  2 C.F.R. 
Parts 200.317 through 200.326 determine if you need to run a 
competitive procurement process. 
 
Generally, the following guidance applies:  
• Micro-Purchase Threshold (MPT): Purchase of supplies and services 
below MPT (currently set at $10,000), does not require 
competitive solicitation. 
• Simplified Acquisition Threshold (SAT): Purchase of supplies and 
services at a price greater than MPT and below SAT (currently set 
at $250,000) requires price or rate quotes from an adequate 
number of vendors. 
• Any purchases above SAT requires a formal procurement process. 
☐ 
2.2 Allowable Costs (not 
applicable to fixed 
amount awards) 
The costs related to the project are only allowed to be charged to the 
grant if they are necessary and reasonable, as described in 2 C.F.R Parts 
200.404 and 200.405, for the performance of the grant program award. 
Confirm all costs would be considered allowable prior to submitting for 
reimbursement.  
 
☐ 
 
 
1 Substantial completion means that the project can fulfill the primary operations that it was designed to perform,  
which is delivering services to end-users. At substantial completion, service operations and management systems 
infrastructure must be operational.

<!-- page 357 -->
Page 3 of 4 
 
2.3 Negotiated Indirect 
Costs Rate Agreement  
(not applicable to fixed 
amount awards) 
Confirm if your organization has a Negotiated Indirect Costs Rate 
Agreement (“NICRA”) OR elect to use the de minimis rate of 10 percent 
of the modified total direct costs pursuant to 2 C.F.R. Part 200.414(f). 
☐ 
 
2.4 Program Income  Ordinarily, program income must be deducted from total allowable 
costs to determine the net allowable costs, as per 2 C.F.R. Part 200.307. 
As per the Treasury’s supplementary guidance issued on May 17, 2023, 
any income generated by subrecipients from subawards will not be 
considered program income and may be used by the ISP without 
constraints.  
 
If the ISP is treated as a contractor, recipients may allow contractors to 
keep any income generated by the broadband infrastructure they 
install, if it's consistent with state procurement requirements or local 
government provisions for procurement, and in this case, such earned 
income by contractors is not considered program income and may be 
used without restrictions. 
 
Income generated from indefeasible rights of use (IRUs) and leases 
associated with broadband infrastructure will not be categorized as 
program income. 
 
☐ 
 
2.5  Prohibition on 
Certain 
Telecommunications 
Equipment 
Confirm grant funds are not used to procure or obtain certain 
telecommunications and video surveillance services or equipment 
provided or produced by designated entities, including certain entities 
owned or controlled by the People’s Republic of China in accordance to 
2 C.F.R. Part 200.216 & 2 C.F.R. Part 200.471.  
☐ 
 
3. Reporting and Compliance  
3.1 Internal Controls Establish effective internal controls to ensure that funding decisions 
constitute eligible uses of funds and document determinations , as per 2 
C.F.R. Part 200.303. 
☐ 
3.2 Financial and 
Performance Reporting 
Financial and performance reporting to ACA Broadband Office is 
required. Refer to the Reporting Checklist for more information on the 
required reporting.  
☐ 
 
3.3 Reporting on Real 
Property 
Report on status of real property that the government retains an 
interest in (unless 15 years or longer) at least annually, as per 2 C.F.R. 
Part 200.330. 
☐ 
 
3.4 Labor Agreements 
and Standards 
If a CPF-funded project receiving $5.0 million or more in CPF funding 
based on total project cost the following must be provided: 
• Certification that all laborers and mechanics employed by 
contractors and subcontractors are paid wages at rates not less than 
those prevailing OR provide a project employment and local impact 
report. 
• Certification that the project includes a project labor agreement, 
meaning a pre-hire collective bargaining agreement OR provide a 
project workforce continuity plan. 
☐

<!-- page 358 -->
Page 4 of 4 
 
3.5 Remedies for 
Noncompliance 
Understand and comply with the terms of the grant agreement and 
applicable state and federal laws, regulations, and guidance. 
Noncompliance may result in withholding or disallowing payments.  
☐ 
 
3.6 Civil Rights Ensure compliance with applicable statutes and regulations prohibiting 
discrimination, including: 
• Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 (42 U.S.C. 2000d et seq.), and 
the U.S. Department of Treasury's implementing regulations, 31 
C.F.R. PART 22 
• Section 504 of the Rehabilitation Act of 1973, as amended (29 
U.S.C. 794)

1681 et seq., and the Department of Justice's implementing

• The Age Discrimination Act of 1975, as amended (42 U.S.C. 6101 et 
seq.), and the U.S. Department of Treasury implementing 
regulations at 31 C.F.R. Part 23

4. Audits and Closeout 
4.1 Closeout  Return any funds not obligated or expended for eligible uses within the 
eligible cost timeframe. 
☐ 
4.2 Audits For subawards,  all non-federal entity subrecipients are subject to the 
audit requirements in subpart F, including single audit or program 
specific audits. For contracts, ISPs are not subject to subpart F of the 
Uniform Guidance with respect to the funds they receive for CPF 
funded board band provides but will be subject to the terms of their 
contracts.  
 
Review the Compliance Supplement for important compliance 
requirements that the federal government expects to be considered as 
a part of such audit. 
 
☐ 
4.3 Access to Records U.S. Department of Treasury has the right to access to any documents, 
papers, or other records pertaining to the federal award, in order to 
make audits. This applies for as long as the records are retained.  
☐ 
4.4 Contract Provisions 
for Non-Federal Entities 
All contracts made by a subrecipient in excess of $100,000 that involve 
the employment of mechanics or laborers must include a provision for 
compliance with certain provisions of the Contract Work Hours and 
Safety Standards Act, 40 U.S.C. 3702 and 3704, as supplemented by 
Department of Labor regulations (29 C.F.R. Part 5). Work in excess of 
the standard work week is permissible provided that the worker is 
compensated at a rate of not less than one and a half times the basic 
rate of pay for all overtime hours. Working conditions must not be 
unsanitary, hazardous, or dangerous. 
☐ 
4.5 Suspension and 
Debarment 
Requirements 
Maintain written standards of conduct and prohibitions on dealing with 
suspended or debarred Parties 2 C.F.R. Part 180

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/27/2023  
Title: Contract #B23-02-015 Engineering Consultant
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract #B23-02-015 Engineering Consultant to HDR Engineering,
Inc. and approval of the contract for Task 1 Pavement Management Program.
Background:
The Engineering Consultant under this contract will provide technical assistance to prepare a
priority planning and development program for the county’s infrastructure projects, such as
drainage, flood control, roadway, traffic, geotech, and bridge projects on a Task Order basis. 
Task 1 Pavement Management Program is to collect pavement condition field data, process the
field data, and  prepare a report that prioritizes the maintenance of the county’s approximate
300 miles of paved roadway.  As part of this task, a GIS geospatial/shapefile with relevant data
will be created for the County’s use.  The pavement management report and other field data
(i.e. ADT information) will help Navajo County prioritize its roadway  maintenance needs.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Government
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T., April 6, 2023. No proposals were accepted
after 3:00 p.m.  The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m. The proposals
were evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request for qualifications. 
Recommendation: 
The Evaluation Committee is recommending award of Contract #B23-02-015 Engineering
Consultant to HDR Engineering, Inc. and approval of the contract for the Task 1 Pavement
Management Program.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2023-24
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 133904.89

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of June 27, 2023, between Navajo County ("County") and 
HDR Engineering, Inc. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certa in professional services for Engineering 
Consultant, Contract No. B23-02-015, Task  1 Pavement Management Program Project 
(“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Work dated 
June 16, 2023, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s schedule and 
fee proposal dated June 16, 2023, in a competent and profes sional manner in 
accordance with this Agreement.  The Scope of Work and the Consultant’s schedule 
are attached hereto and by this reference incorporated herein.  If any incorporated 
term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control.   
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior 
written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be with held from any payment by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 366 -->
Page -2- 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is  responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if  necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  County shall pay to Consultant a lump sum of one hundred thirty-three 
thousand nine hundred four dollars and eighty-nine cents ($133,904.89) as payment in 
full for all services rendered by Consultant  pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments 
shall be made within 30 days of County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  
Each invoice shall detail the work performed during the billing period.  Invoice 
amounts in percent of Project cost shall not exceed the percentage of completion of 
the Project as approved by County.   
 
7. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
8. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect , at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of 
Insurance naming County as  an additional insured (except for Errors and 
Omissions / Professional Liability and Workers Compensation coverage). 
 
 
B. The following types an d amounts of insurance are required as minimums:

Standard minimum deductibles are allowed.  Any deductibles are the

be treated as a material breach by County.  Consultant shall maintain

the Project. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
9. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable  by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless County and its  representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to reasonable attorney fees, court costs, and 
the costs of appellate proceedings) to the extent caused by and relating to, arising out 
of or resulting from Consultant’s negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the 
performance of this Agreement. Consultant ’s duty to defend, hold harmless and 
indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise in connection with any claim, damage, loss or 
expense that is attributable to bodily inju ry, sickness, disease, death or injury to, 
impairment or destruction of property, to  the extent caused by any negligent act, 
error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those by 
any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may be 
liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negl igence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
10. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to time-based fees or 
reimbursable expenses invoiced to County under this Agreement for a period of five 
years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be subject to inspection 
and audit by County upon reasonable notice during normal business hours.  A legible 
copy of any or all such documents shall be produced by Consultant at the request and 
expense of County.

<!-- page 368 -->
Page -4- 
 
11. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without County’s prior written approval, nor  shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.   
 
12. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting doc umentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
13. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   County may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by County as a result of Cons ultant’s default), C onsultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
reasonable difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written n otice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it 
is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided.

<!-- page 369 -->
Page -5- 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all servic es (unless the notice directs otherwise), 
and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to County copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and other 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement.  Re-use of Consu ltant’s deliverables for purposes not 
contemplated in the Scope of Work sha ll be at County’s sole risk and without 
liability to Consultant. 
 
14. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act  of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also 
terminate this Agreement upon written notice  and a ten-day opportunity to cure if 
County fails to make any payment within the time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant 
may also terminate this Ag reement upon written notice if  suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
15. Governing Law.   This Agreement shall  be governed by the law of the State  of 
Arizona.  Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of 
Arizona (with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only 
after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 12. 
 
16. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included 
herein.  Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such 
provision has been omitted. 
 
17. Non-Collusion.  The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference. 
 
18. One-Year Limitation on Actions.   No legal cause of action shall be initiated by 
Consultant on any claim based upon or aris ing out of this Agreement unless such 
action is commenced within one year after County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
19. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is valid for two (2) years from the date 
the agreement was made between the two parties and shall continue month-to-month 
thereafter unless and until terminated by either of the parties in accordance with the 
terms of this Agreement. Upon mutual agreement between the parties, this contract 
may be renewed up to four (4) additional one-year terms. 
 
20. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between  the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.

be invalid or unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other

NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT HOLBROOK, AZ SCOPEForNAVAJO COUNTY ENGINEERING CONSULTANT (B 23-02-15) TASK 1 – PAVEMENT PROGRAM MANGEMENTHDR Engineering, Inc.JUNE 16, 2023The purpose of this task is to collect pavement condition field data, process the field data, and prepare a report that prioritizes the maintenance of the county’s approximate 300 miles of paved roadway. As part of this task, a GIS geospatial/shapefile with relevant data will be created for the county’s use. The Pavement Management Report and other field data (i.e. ADT information) will help the Navajo County (NC) prioritize its roadway maintenance needs.    SCOPE OF WORK Task 1.0 Project Management:  This work includes monitoring the budget, overseeing project progress, establishing the project execution timeline, coordination with vendor, developing and coordinating scope of works, and submitting monthly status reports and invoices to the NC Public Works Department Project Manager. Task 2.0 Meetings:  This work involves attending meetings, provide meetings agendas and document meeting minutes. It is anticipated that a total of 6 meetings will be needed for this project as detailed in the included estimate. The Kick-Off-Meeting (KOM) will start the project and discuss the pavement field assessment approach. It is assumed all meetings will be conducted virtually and no in-person is required. Task 3.0 Pavement Evaluation Report:  HDR will review the pavement condition assessment spreadsheet-based data summary and report provided by IMS, which will detail the types, quantities and severity of the identified types of pavement distress and the Pavement Condition Index (PCI) rating determined for each surveyed roadway/roadway segment. HDR will evaluate the PCI ratings and develop a report the defines the prioritized approach to preventive maintenance and rehabilitation (M&R) or reconstruction based on the pavement condition (PCI rating) and considering Average Daily Traffic, IMS recommended M&R, and other factors as appropriate and agreed upon with NC Project Manager.  The report will prioritize and rank the roadways/roadway segments based on the PCI and other factors agreed upon with NC Project Manager. M&R activities to be considered will be consistent with County standards and practices and considering applicable ADOT design standards, and will include: crack sealing; fog sealing; chip sealing; slurry sealing; overlay/mill and overlay; full-depth reconstruction. A compiled summary report of the findings will be prepared including the M&R activities and estimated costs, the IMS report and spreadsheet file of PCI data, and pavement images in supporting files. An Initial Pavement Management Report will be submitted for review and comments. Comments will be addressed and resolved appropriately. I final report will be submitted that incorporates the review comments from the Initial report. Task 4.0 Cost Estimating:  Estimated costs per lane-mile data for the various M&R activities will be developed using unit costs obtained from available previous bid tabs for projects within the

<!-- page 376 -->
CITY OF PHOENIX STREET TRANSPORTATION DEPARTMENTBRIDGE & DAM SAFETY SECTION   
2
Navajo County region and the roadway pavement areas from the IMS data. Budgeting level costs (15% estimates) will be developed for each roadway/roadway segment for the selected M&R activity and included in the report. Adjustments for the year of expenditure will be used. The estimates will be developed for up to seven M&R options. Task 5.0 Geographic Information System (GIS):  HDR will filter the roads in the geospatial road data provided by Navajo County to those meeting the county’s criteria for paved roadway. That subset of geospatial data will then be shared with the sub-contractor as a geodatabase for them to augment with their field collection information. Once collected, the enhanced geodatabase will be shared with HDR so that further analysis can be performed, and additional information added. The deliverable to NC Project Manager will be a fully robust geospatial dataset in both geodatabase and shapefile formats. Task 6.0 Quality Control/Quality Assurance:  The work will include reviewing the initial and final reports and GIS files for conformity, accuracy and meeting the task order SOW and objectives. Schedule:  This schedule is based on starting work on receipt of notice to proceed (NTP). -Field work and Data Processing: 130 calendar days after NTP (Assumes no roadway restriction due to snowfall)  -Initial Pavement Management Report to NC for review: 85 calendar days after obtaining field data from IMS  -Final Pavement Management Report to NC: 45 calendar days after comments (Assumes 21-day review period for NC Public Works Department to provide comments on the Initial Report)

Pavement Condition SurveyOur two-person field crews will collect both outward facing and downward facing pavement imagery, using one of our RSTs equipped with LCMS-2 3D pavement imaging technology. Pavement surface distresses including load cracking, block cracking, rutting, raveling, reflective cracking, loss of section, bleeding, edge distress, and patched areas as well as right-of-way imagery will be collected on a segment-by-segment basis, with each distress being captured by type, extent, and severity. The data and imagery that is collected is then linked to the CountyThe LCMS-2 system is the highest resolution 3D pavement scanning technology available. Each LCMS-2 system relies on two downward-facing, high-resolution 3D cameras. Combined, the two 3D cameras capture continuous downward imagery for more than a standard lane width. The cameras are coupled with downward-facing lasers that provide constant and consistent illumination of the pavement surface regardless of ambient lighting conditions. The impacts of shadows from trees, buildings, or simply overcast sky conditions are eliminated by the laser illumination.
The 3D cameras can detect one-millimeter-wide cracks and full-lane-width rutting, as required by ASTM D6433, on the pavement surface at speeds up to 65 mph. Due to the versatility of the LCMS-2 technology, the automated pavement condition survey will be performed at posted speeds, and traffic control will not be necessary for the data collection effort. Pavement data collection and imagery surveys are expected to progress at a rate of between 35 and 50 miles per day for the County. 
IMS Road Surface Tester (RST) equipped with Laser Crack Measurement System (LCMS-2)(Note: IMS has five RST LCMS-2 equipped systems dedicated to municipal pavement management.)

<!-- page 382 -->
Navajo County, AZPavement Management Services
IMS Infrastructure Management Services, LP 5
The IMS team then processes the collected data using a combination of advanced analytical tools and determine accurate and repeatable PCI values for each roadway segment. Furthermore, we deliver our PCI ratings and supporting data (distress information, rutting, and IRI values) in both spreadsheet and GIS formats for easy review. The data that we provide may be used immediately for decision making or be imported into any pavement management system.Any distresses that are not captured by the automated LCMS-2 system will be captured by our trained in-vehicle distress raters. The automated data is supplemented by information collected by the second crew person in the van using a mobile mapping solution known as NOMADTM. The NOMADfile format is part of unique approach to pavement condition surveys. The NOMADfiles contain useful information for our field crews, including direction routing information and one-pass versus two-pass data collection instructions, and allow our trained field staff to capture additional condition and inspection information that is used by our QC/QA team to validate condition data.During data collection, IMS implements routines that are performed each day of data collection to ensure data consistency. These include:Equipment is calibrated, and daily reports are completed.All sensors are continually monitored to ensure they are receiving data within specification.The Crew Chief and operator manually monitor the HD digital images, GPS, distress recorder,roughness measurements, and rutting data.Each street is noted on the inventory and map, as well as through GPS and assignment of the RST van number.Production is tracked and records of coverage are documented. A corrective action plan is followed, as necessary.All data is backed up and sent to the IMS main office for processing.ASTM D6433 Pavement Condition EvaluationDuring and following the data collection effort, our team will evaluate all collected pavement imagery and surface measurements to arrive at Pavement Condition Index (PCI) values. This is a six-step process that includes the following tasks:1.RoadInspectPavement Distress Detection Cracks, rutting, and other pavement distresses are automatically detected in both the 2D and 3D pavement images. IRI values are also calculated at this time. (Note: The RoadInspectsoftware was developed by Pavemetrics, the firm that manufactures the LCMS-2 technology.)2.IMS Pavement Distress Classification Pavement distresses detected by the RoadInspectsoftware are then classified by type (e.g., alligator cracking, bleeding, edge cracking, etc.) and 
interface for entering notes and supplemental field data.
LCMS-2 cracking data in QC/QA review stage.

Navajo County, AZPavement Management Services
IMS Infrastructure Management Services, LP 6
severity (e.g., low, medium, or high) based on predefined criteria (e.g., ASTM D6433). IMS has created a suite of custom tools that include rule-based algorithms in conjunction with artificial intelligence to accurately classify pavement distresses.3.IMS PCI CalculationPCI values are determined for each roadway segment using a scale from zero (0) to one hundred (100) as defined in ASTM D6433.4.IMS Quality Control (QC)The IMS project manager then does a review of the data and works with the QC team lead to address any issues that may be identified. 5.IMS Quality Assurance (QA)The IMS project QA Manager independently reviews the rated data and works with our Project Manager and QC team to correct any issues observed. 6.Client QAThe IMS Project Manager reviews the findings of the pavement condition data with County staff before beginning any analysis activities. IMS will present the pavement condition data in a Client Review Spreadsheet (CRS) along with maps illustrating pavement conditions for the client to independently review.Rutting and Roughness-2 systems detect rutting on asphalt roadways using laser measurements of transverse profiles that are collected continuously as the vans drive at normal traffic speed. With more than 4,000 measurement points collected per transverse profile and sub-millimeter vertical accuracy, the LCMS-2 can define transverse profiles with a high level of precision, accuracy, and repeatability.Once all the transverse profiles are collected for a roadway, IMS uses the Brazilian Method, which is an industry standard method, to determine the rut area and the deepest (or maximum) rut depth for the profiles. These values, as well as width of rut and color coding, can be seen in the LCMS-2 laser image to the right. Red represents high severity rutting, while orange represents moderate severity rutting. The severities are determined based on maximum rut depth thresholds that are specified in ASTM D6433.Pavement roughness is evaluated by measuring the accumulated difference in the vertical displacement of a road surface, independent of chassis response, over a prescribed road length (longitudinal profile). This roughness is typically reported via theInternational Roughness Index (IRI). IRI data is calculated in real time from continuous longitudinal profile data collected by the LCMS-device. To determine the road profile, data is simultaneously obtained from three devices: a pulse transducer-based distance-measuring instrument (DMI), high-speed 3D laser sensors operating at 112 MHz, and an accelerometer in compliance with ASTM E 950. The LCMS-2 unit conforms-valid roadway sections such as localized maintenance activities, railroad crossings, speed bumps, andbrick inlays and not affect the IRI value.
Rut area and severity levels shown on the LCMS-2 composite image.

Quality Control and Quality AssuranceIMS has developed a unique approach to pavement condition assessments by coupling manual review of distress data with automated algorithms. This more rigorous QC/QA process ensures that the automated distress detection and classification algorithms that we use work correctly. Our Pavement Engineering team fully understands the capabilities and limitations of the state-of-the-art LCMS-2 technologies and sophisticated algorithms that we employ, and our QC/QA steps are extremely important in ensuring the success of the project.The IMS QC/QA process is comprehensive and makes use of field observations, automated data processing tools, manual data review by our QC team, independent review by our QA manager, and lastly, final review by the County. There are QC/QA checks at each stage of the project to ensure data quality before the data is moved into the next steps.Machine learning and artificial intelligence have made leaps and bounds in speeding up distress identification. Title: Concho Highway Final Design, Contract No. B23-02-003
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director

Department: Public Works
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract B23-02-003 Concho Highway Final Design to Kimley-Horn
and Associates, Inc.
Background:
This project will rehabilitate pavement on Concho Highway, the County’s major arterial
connecting Snowflake and Apache County.  The Consultant is responsible for ensuring the final
design of the project is developed in accordance with federal, state, tribal, and local regulations,
statutes, policies, procedures, and guidelines.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Government
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T.,March 15, 2023. No proposals were accepted
after 3:00 p.m.  The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m. The proposals
were evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request for qualifications. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends award of contract B23-02-003 Concho Highway Final Design to
Kimley-Horn and Associates, Inc.

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
 
AGREEMENT made as of June 27, 2023, between Navajo County ("County") and 
Kimley-Horn and Associates, Inc. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain professional services for Concho Highway Final 
Design, Contract No. B23-02-003 (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Services 
identified in June 19,2023, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s 
schedule and fee proposal identified in June 19, 2023, in a competent and professional 
manner to the satisfaction of County.  Th e Scope of Services and the Consultant’s 
schedule and fee proposal are attached he reto and by this reference incorporated 
herein. 
 
2. Contract Documents.
 The Contract Documents comprise this Contract, Request for 
Qualifications No. B23-02 -003, Proposal Submittal date d March 8, 2023, June 
19,2023 Scope of Services, June 19, 2023 Consultants Schedule and Fee Proposal, 
and addenda thereto, if any. If any incorporated term is inconsistent with the Contract, 
this Contract shall control. 
 
3. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior 
written approval.  
 
4. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be with held from any payment by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 395 -->
Page -2- 
 
5. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 9, 
10, 11, 13, 16, 19, 22 and 23.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
6. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if  necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
7. Payment.  County shall pay to Consultant a not-to-exceed sum of Ninety thousand 
twenty two dollars and fifty cents ($90,022. 50) as payment in full for all services 
rendered by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 
30 days of County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall 
detail the work performed during the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of 
Project cost shall not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as approved 
by County.   
 
8. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 13.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
9. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect , at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as an 
additional insured (except for Errors and Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage).

A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend,

from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the

a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by County as a result of Cons ultant’s default), C onsultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written n otice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it 
is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable

services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant

Concho Highway Scoping and Final Design 
Project No. B23-02-003 
June 19, 2023 
SCOPE OF SERVICES 
Project Overview 
This pavement rehabilitation project is located along Concho Highway starting at the Town 
of Snowflake, AZ limits on the west end and extending east to the Navajo/Apache County 
line. The project consists of rehabilitating the existing pavement, including m illing and filling 
the asphaltic concrete surface from the Town of Snowflake Limits to Concho Ocho Road. The 
project also includes preliminary design for adding turn lanes at the White Antelope Road and 
Hay Hollow Road intersections. Finally, the project a lso includes preliminary design for 
shoulder widening throughout the project limits. 
The project is county funded and will not require NEPA level environmental clearance.   
Task 1 – Schedule and Meetings 
Following notice to proceed (NTP), Kimley-Horn will submit a detailed project schedule clearly 
identifying all major tasks and primary submittal dates/milestones. The schedule will show 
the anticipated completion dates for each task of the project.  The anticipated advertisement 
date for this project is early 4th Quarter of FY 2024 (April of 2024). 
Kimley-Horn will schedule, prepare and attend one (1) in-person project kick-off meeting and 
three (3) virtual monthly progress  meetings. Progress meetings will be combined with 
comment resolution meetings at each staged submittal.  A total of  four (4) meetings are 
included. Kimley-Horn will prepare project team list, a sign -in-sheet, agenda and meeting 
notes for all meetings.   
Coordination with Navajo County, sub-consultants and outside agencies will be needed.  A 
project team will be set up to include all the necessary County contacts and outside agencies.  
Task 2 – Data Collection 
Kimley-Horn will collect and review existing as -built plans and available data .  One (1) field

<!-- page 403 -->
Page 3 
kimley-horn.com 7740 N. 16th Street, Suite 300, Phoenix, AZ 85020 602 944 5500 
 
reviews will be conducted during the development process . Field review # 1 will be  to 
inventory project details, confirm design approach, and verify survey data.   
Task 3 – Scoping Documents 
Kimley-Horn will prepare a brief Scoping Memo for the project.  
The Scoping Memo will provide a brief overview of the alternatives evaluate d and 
recommendations. A thorough scoping memo that conforms to ADOT Predesign 
requirements is not anticipated and is not included in this scope of services.  
An initial Scoping Memo will be submitted for comments. Comments will be compiled and 
addressed. One (1) comment resolution meeting is also anticipated to resolve comments. 
Final Scoping Memo will be prepared and submitted. 
The following scope items will be designed to a conceptual level. Design and plans beyond 
this level for these items is not included in this scope of services. 
• Shoulder widening throughout the project limits with centerline and edge line rumble 
strips 
• Adding left turn lanes for both eastbound and westbound traffic and a right turn lane 
for westbound traffic at White Antelope Road 
• Adding left turn lanes for both eastbound and westbound traffic at Hay Hollow Road 
• Upgrading existing guardrail to Manual for Assessing Safety Hardware (MASH) 
Standards and installing new guardrail as needed to meet the requireme nts of 
American Association of State and Highway Transportation Officials (AASHTO) 
Roadside Design Guide (4th Edition, 2011). 
• Installing concrete barrier at the existing Concho Flat Wash box culvert including 
guardrail on the approaches. 
The County previously developed a conceptual design for reprofiling Concho Highway at the 
new Pit Road immediately west of G aribaldi Road. Kimley -Horn will review the design and 
prepare a preliminary opinion of probable cost for the County’s conceptual design. Additional 
design for the reprofiling of Concho Highway and associated impacts is not included in this 
scope of services. 
Kimley-Horn will assist the County in preparing an ADOT Local Public Agency (LPA) Project 
Initiation Letter for the above listed improvements and will perform the following services: 
• Prepare preliminary opinion of probable cost

<!-- page 404 -->
Page 4 
kimley-horn.com 7740 N. 16th Street, Suite 300, Phoenix, AZ 85020 602 944 5500 
 
• Perform a brief crash analysis to determine crash modification factors (CMF) in 
accordance with Part D of the Highway Safety Manual. 
• Prepare benefit-cost analysis in accordance with ADOT guidelines 
Kimley-Horn will assist Navajo County with preparing one application for Highway Safety 
Improvement Program (HSIP) funding for the above-mentioned improvements. 
Earthwork will be quantified under a square yardage item. Three -dimensional modeling will 
not be performed. An earthwork report will not be prepared. 
Task 4 – Survey and Mapping 
Kimley-Horn, through their sub-consultant Painted Sky Engineering and Survey, will perform 
design level survey to establish the roadway centerline . (See Painted Sky Engineering and 
Survey’s Scope of Services). We will also review and coordinate with Painted Sky Engineering 
and Survey. Topographic survey and aerial photogrammetry are not included in this scope of 
services. 
Task 5 – Geotechnical and Pavement Design  
Kimley-Horn, through their sub -consultant Ethos, will perform geotechnical investigations 
and prepare a Geotechnical and Pavement Design Report as required for pavement design. 
(See Ethos’s Scope of Services).  
Task 6 – Environmental Studies 
It is assumed the improvements that will be carried forward to final design and construction 
will be limited to pavement rehabilitation with no ground disturbance. It is also assumed that 
construction will not include any federal funding. Thus, no environm ental studies are 
required. Environmental studies are not included in this scope of services.   
Task 7 – Utility Locating and Coordination 
Kimley-Horn will research and obtain private utility locating maps that are available for the 
project limits. Kimley -Horn will contact Arizona 811 (Blue Stake), communicate and 
coordinate with utility companies, obtain as-built information, generate existing utilities into 
the base maps, indicate existing utilities on plan exhibits , determine and resolve utility 
conflicts, and prepare utility special provisions and clearance.  
It is anticipated that no utility designating, potholing or utility relocations will be required .

<!-- page 405 -->
Page 5 
kimley-horn.com 7740 N. 16th Street, Suite 300, Phoenix, AZ 85020 602 944 5500 
 
These services are not included in this scope of services. 
Kimley-Horn will prepare the utility special provisions and coordinate with Navajo County to 
obtain the utility clearance. 
Task 8 – Roadway Design  
Kimley-Horn will prepare final design for the following specific improvements: 
• Pavement rehabilitation from the Town of Snowflake Limits to C oncho Ocho R oad 
(approximately 5.0 miles) 
Stage submittals will include Preliminary (60%), and Final (100%) and will consist of the 
following: 
• One face sheet (General notes, Index of sheets, Benchmark, Vicinity map, and County 
Map) 
• One Typical Section Sheet 
• One turnout detail sheet 
• One survey control sheet 
• One Key Map sheet at 500-scale – Double Banked 
Kimley-Horn will develop a best fit horizontal alignment utilizing survey data provided by 
Kimley-Horn’s Subconsultant, Painted Sky Engineering a nd Survey . The existing horizontal 
alignment will be maintained. Geometric centerline layout information will be shown the Key 
Map sheet.  
Pavement rehabilitation  improvements will be shown on typical sections and details. 
Separate roadway plan sheets will NOT be prepared. 
The plans will be updated with comments received, addressed, and submitted. 
No earthwork is anticipated. An earthwork report will not be prepared. Cross sections will not 
be prepared. 
Task 9 – Drainage Design 
Existing drainage patterns will be maintained. Impacts to existing drainage facilities are not 
anticipated. Drainage analysis will not be performed. Drainage sheets and memos will not be 
prepared.

<!-- page 406 -->
Page 6 
kimley-horn.com 7740 N. 16th Street, Suite 300, Phoenix, AZ 85020 602 944 5500 
 
Task 10 – Erosion Control 
It is anticipated that the contractor will be responsible for obtaining the necessary permits 
for erosion control, including but not limited to, a Stormwater Pollution Prevention Plan 
(SWPPP) and Notice of Intent (NOI) for the Arizona Pollutant Discharge Elimination System 
(AZPDES).  
Erosion control design is not included in this scope of services. 
Task 11 – Structures Design 
Structural design is not included in this scope of services. 
Task 12 – Traffic Design 
Task 12.1 – Maintenance of Traffic (MOT) 
Kimley-Horn will develop a construction schedule to establish the contract time and will 
prepare traffic control special provisions including liquidated damages for lane closures. A 
Road User Cost analysis wi ll be performed to determine any liquidated damages. The 
liquidated damages, construction schedule, and associated contract time will be developed 
with concurrence from County staff.   
MOT plan sheets are not included in this scope of services: 
Kimley-Horn will develop quantities for the traffic control items, which will be quantified in a 
lump sum traffic control item. 
Task 12.2 – Signing 
Kimley-Horn assumes that the roadway widening portion of the project will not be carried 
through to construction with this contract thus existing signing will not be impacted. Signing 
design is excluded from this scope of services.  
Task 12.3 – Pavement Marking 
Pavement marking will be addressed through special provisions and will require the 
contractor to perform an inventory and as-build the existing pavement markings and replace 
in kind. Pavement marking plan sheets are not included in this scope of services. Kimley-Horn 
will prepare quantities and cost estimates for pavement marking.

<!-- page 407 -->
Page 7 
kimley-horn.com 7740 N. 16th Street, Suite 300, Phoenix, AZ 85020 602 944 5500 
 
Task 13 – Right-of-Way Determination 
Navajo County will provide existing Right-of-Way ( R/W) information along the project 
extents.  Kimley-Horn will verify and confirm the existing R/W to provide in the construction 
documents.  It is anticipated that temporary constructions easements (TCEs) will  not be 
required for the improvements included in this scope of services. 
Task 14 – Cost Estimates 
Kimley-Horn will prepare engineer’s opinion of probable cost estimates for each design 
submittal starting with the preliminary design submittal and followed by the final submittal.  
Kimley-Horn will prepare quantities at each design submittal using standard measurement 
and payment methods.  Kimley -Horn will provide estimated unit costs based on recent bid 
tabulations and historic bid database information available from the ADOT website.  
Task 15 – Specifications 
Kimley-Horn will develop the project specifications. The ADOT 2021 Standard Specifications 
for Roadway and Bridge Construction and associated Stored Specifications will be the basis 
for the specifications. Kimley-Horn will prepare Special Provi sions for items, details and 
procedures not adequately covered by ADOT’s Standard Specifications and Stored 
Specifications.  Special Provisions will be submitted starting with the preliminary design 
submittal and followed by the final submittal.  
Kimley-Horn will coordinate with the project team for preparation and submittal of project 
specifications, special provisions and opinions of probable construction costs at each 
submittal. 
Task 16 – Bid Assistance 
Kimley-Horn will provide support throughout the bidding period as requested by Navajo 
County for up to the hours as shown in the derivation of fee proposal person-hour estimate.  
Kimley-Horn will participate in the prebid, prepare addendums, and respond to  requests for 
information on an as needed basis up to the specified number of hours.  
Task 17 – Contract Administration 
It is anticipated that the project design will be completed within 12 months after receiving 
Notice-to-Proceed (NTP). Kimley-Horn will provide project management and administration 
services for the project.  Services will include invoicing, administrative services, internal

<!-- page 408 -->
Page 8 
kimley-horn.com 7740 N. 16th Street, Suite 300, Phoenix, AZ 85020 602 944 5500 
 
schedule, quality assurance, and quality control.  
Kimley-Horn will distribute project deliverables at each submittal. Submittals will start with 
the preliminary design submittal and followed by the final submittal  . Each submittal will be 
distributed to all team members identified.  Comments received on each submittal will be 
compiled, addressed, and discussed at comment resolution meetings to be held in 
conjunction with progress meetings. 
Additional Services 
Items not covered by the preceding scope shall be considered additional services. Kimley -
Horn will provide additional services upon receipt of written authorization from you pending 
agreement between Navajo County  and Kimley -Horn regarding scope, cost and schedule.  
Additional services could include, but are not limited to, the following: 
1. Additional survey 
2. Environmental Studies 
3. Public meeting 
4. Additional Meetings and Coordination 
5. Final Design for scope items specifically listed under the Scoping Documents Task 
6. Final Design for scope items beyond those specifically listed und er the Roadw ay 
Design Task 
7. Detailed crash analysis using Part C of the Highway Safety Manual 
8. Post design services
