Dear Taylor: 
As requested, Ethos Engineering, LLC (Ethos) is ple ased to present this scope of work, 
work-hour estimate, and fee proposal for profession al geotechnical engineering services 
for the referenced project to the Kimley-Horn & Ass ociates, Inc. (KHA) design team.  
This revision addresses a reduction in scope (total  of 5 miles) from the Navajo County 
(County). 
1.0 Purpose 
Concho highway is the County’s major rural arterial  connecting Snowflake and Apache 
County. The project will involve pavement rehabilit ation with a widened shoulder and 
spot improvements at various locations. The project  will include intersection 
improvements at two locations: White Antelope Road,  and Hay Hollow Road and 
modernization of traffic safety elements such as wa rning & regulatory signs, striping and 
guardrails. The project will include drainage improvements at various locations within the 
5-mile segment of the roadway. 
The purpose of the geotechnical engineering service s will be to evaluate the physical 
properties of the existing pavement conditions to e stablish engineering criteria for the 
final design and construction of the roadway improv ements.  ur prpsed scpe f 
wr fr getechica services is based on the Request for Qualifications (2023), a 
Scoping Meeting and discussion documented herein. 
The contracting method for this work is lump sum.  We will utilize various lab and drilling 
subcontractors to complete this work with an emphas is placed on using Disadvantaged 
Business Enterprise (DBE) firms to the extent possible. 
 
2.0 Geotechnical Scope of Services 
Based on the information provided by KHA, we unders tand pavement cores will need to 
be taken along Concho Hwy to determine if there is sufficient pavement structure for the 
proposed improvements.  No geotechnical reports were made available. 
Ethos will perform a subsurface investigation of th e existing site soils with as-needed 
engineering support to be provided by ACS Services,  LLC (ACS).  The following 
describes the geotechnical scope of work planned an d assumptions made to form the

<!-- page 420 -->
June 12, 2023 
Concho highway Improvements – Geotechnical Scope an d Fee Proposal 
Contract B23-02-003 
2 
 
 
basis of our proposal.  In general, the scope of wo rk will include field exploration 
including pavement cores followed by engineering analyses, and reporting. 
2.1 Project Initiation 
Ethos will show intended core locations, equipment access, and equipment information. 
We will also coordinate with the County for approva l to work within the County right-of-
way. We will coordinate the coring, and traffic con trol subcontractors to schedule the 
field work. We have assumed Ethos will initially ma rk the cre catis After the cre 
catis are ared we wi crdiate with Ari a 811 t cate pubic udergrud 
utiities that ay be preset  the site  It is understood that upon approval of the FIP, a 
no-fee encroachment permit will be required from the County.  
It is assumed that no additional permit fees or rig ht-of-way permits will be required from 
additional entities (i.e. City of Snowflake). Our p roposal does not include any effort 
associated with obtaining these items.  
2.2 Geotechnical Field Investigation 
 
Ethos will conduct geotechnical explorations for th e roadway elements to evaluate the 
pavement conditions at the site.  Ethos will perform a visual assessment in the vicinity of 
the proposed improvements to evaluate and quantify existing pavement distress 
conditions within the project limits.  The survey w ill be necessary to determine whether 
the existing pavement is suitable for the proposed pavement treatment. 
Our drilling exploration program includes: 
Roadway Cores : The project will include 3 roadway cores (one app roximately 
every 1.5 miles) along the alignment that will penetrate the pavement.  The roadway 
cores will be completed with a hand-held coring rig to evaluate pavement conditions 
for the proposed improvements.  It is assumed that all cores will be completed on 
the existing pavement.  Upon completion of the fiel d activities, the cores will be 
backfilled with cold patch. 
An Ethos field engineer will oversee the coring ope rations. No soil or asphalt samples 
will be obtained from the test cores as laboratory testing is not anticipated. 
We plan on using lane closures with flaggers for ou r field work along Concho highway.  
We will submit traffic control plans for the various traffic control setups. 
2.3 Engineering Analysis & Reporting 
Ethos will perform engineering analyses of the data  obtained from the explorations to 
prepare a design report for the project. The report will be prepared under the supervision 
of a Professional Civil Engineer registered in the State of Arizona. We plan on preparing 
the a  draft and final Geotechnical Roadway Report addressing pavement.

<!-- page 421 -->

Generally, the Geotechnical report will include the following data: 
• Description of the area covered by the report and existing site conditions, 
including vicinity map. 
• Description of the geology and topography of the a rea, including soil and rock 
types, and drainage characteristics and estimated depth to groundwater. 
• A site plan showing core locations and a descripti on of procedures and 
equipment used in the subsurface investigation program. 
• A discussion of geological and geotechnical condit ions and results with reference 
to specific locations on the Project. 
• Results of visual pavement assessment. 
• Recommendations for pavement improvements. 
 
2.4 Project Meetings and Management  
Our understanding is that the Ethos project manager  will attend the project kickoff 
meeting. We have assumed that a meeting at one stag e submittal to discuss 
geotechnical considerations will be required.  We h ave assumed all meetings will be via 
teleconference.  We have further assumed regular at tendance at the monthly progress 
meetings will not be necessary.

Scope of Work 
Painted Sky Engineering & Survey, LLC shall provide the Services and Deliverable(s) as follows:  
Task 1 – Establish Survey Control 
A Painted Sky Engineering and Survey, LLC (PSES)  survey crew will establish survey control along Concho 
Highway between the Town of Snowflake limits and the Navajo County line. Survey control will be set up 
in the Arizona East State Plan coordinate system. Control shall be established at  the beginning and end 
of the project and near the intersections with White Antelope Road and Hay Hollow Road. Control will 
also be established at 1.5-mile intervals over the length of the project and reference monuments shall be 
set in the right-of-way. PSES shall run several OPUS solutions along the length of the project to ensure that 
the project has good elevations and coordinates within the State Plane coordinate system.  
 
Task 2 – Centerline Survey 
A PSES survey crew shall locate the centerline of Concho Highway between the Town of Snowflake limits 
and the Navajo County line. The surveyors shall use GPS to take shots on the approximate points of 
curvature and tangency and shots approximately every 200 feet along any curves and 500 feet along 
any tangents between curves . The survey crew will  also take GPS shots at all locations where the 
centerline markings change. These centerline shots shall be tied into the survey control established as a 
part of Task 1. All survey shots shall be compiled into a single AutoCAD file and provided to the client.  
 
Task 3 – Project Management 
This task accounts for communication and coordination with Kimley -Horn and Navajo County, as well as 
interdepartmental communication throughout the duration of the project.  
 
PSES proposes to provide these services on a Lump Sum basis for a fee of $ 10,241.00. These services will 
be invoiced monthly, based on the percentage of work that is completed each month.  
 
Exclusions: 
Not included in this scope of services, but can be negotiated includes:  
• Aerial Survey 
• Boundary Survey 
• Record of Survey 
• Construction Staking 
• Engineering Design

Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of Amendment No. 1 to the Pavement Markings Contract
#B22-06-028 extending the contract one (1) additional year.
Background:
On September 13, 2022, the Board of Supervisors approved the contract with Arizona Lines,
LLC. for Pavement Markings Contract #B22-06-028. Three bids were received and Arizona
Lines, LLC. was the lowest responsible responsive bid received. This contract has worked very
well for Navajo County and upon written mutual agreement, Navajo County may renew the
contract for up to four (4) additional one-year terms. This would be the first renewal and
concurrence that has been received by the contractor.

Subject:
Presentation to Brad Carlyon on receiving the Michael C. Cudahy Criminal Justice Award from
the State Bar of Arizona
Background: 
The State Bar of Arizona awarded Navajo County Attorney Brad Carlyon with the
distinguished Michael C. Cudahy Criminal Justice Award, which recognizes criminal law
prosecutors who have worked tirelessly to advance the principles of criminal justice by
representing the public's interest with integrity, fairness, tenacity, creativity, brilliance and,
above all, professionalism.  The award winner is selected by the Bar's Board of Governors.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. b.        
Meeting Date: 06/27/2023  
Title: MMC Certification
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Deputy Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Chairman Alberto Peshlakai
Subject:
Presentation to Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board,on receiving her MMC Certification 
Background: 
Melissa received her MMC Certification in May 2023.  The MMC program is the second and
more advanced of the two professional designations granted by IIMC. The MMC program is an
advanced continuing education program that prepares participants to perform complex
municipal duties. The program has an extensive and rigorous educational component and a
professional contribution component . The MMC applicant must demonstrate that they have
actively pursued education and professional activities.
